ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-04-sejm-66-2.u4	Dear Marshal! Prime Minister! Ministers! Mr. President of the Supreme Chamber of Control! Mr. President of the National Bank of Poland! Dear guests! High Seym! I have the honour to present to the High House a report by the Committee on Public Finances on the report on the implementation of the State budget for the period from 1 January to 31 December 2017, together with a proposal on discharge to the Council of Ministers for that period. The state budget for 2017 was the first author's budget of the Government of Law and Justice of the Sejm, prepared under the direction of Prime Minister Beata Szydło and Deputy Prime Minister, Minister of Finance Mateusz Morawiecki. I have a personal satisfaction that this report concerns a very well-designed and implemented budget, which has served Poles and Poland well last year. [[Applause]] It was a groundbreaking budget in many ways. In 2017, the gross domestic product at constant prices amounted to 4.6% against the planned amount of 3.6%. At the same time, it was 2.2 percentage points higher than in the European Union, where it was only 2.4%. It was also higher than global GDP growth of 3.8%. This growth was not an increase of one year, the Polish budget, Polish GDP has a growth trend. In the first quarter of this year, it grew most throughout the European Union, counting quarter by quarter according to Eurostat data. Compared to the fourth quarter of 2017, Polish GDP increased by 1.6%. According to EU statistics, our economy grew to 5.2% in the first quarter of 2018. The Treasury debt decreased from 50% in 2016 to 46.8% GDP at the end of 2017. Gross domestic product in 2017 amounted to PLN 1 982 100 million. This year, the three largest rating agencies raised the perspective of assessing the creditworthiness of Poland. The positive perspective reflects solid economic and fiscal performance of Poland and points to an increased likelihood of raising Poland's rating in the coming months. Public debt has fallen to 48.5% GDP of 51.9% GDP in 2016 The deficit of the public finance sector, calculated according to national methodology, decreased from 2,5% GDP in 2016 to only 0.7% GDP in 2017 The general government deficit, calculated according to the EU methodology, has also been reduced ESA 2010. In 2017, it amounted to PLN 33 billion and amounted to 1.7% GDP. This phenomenon is worth noting that the sector's deficit has taken place under the conditions of broad social policy programmes, including the 500+ programme or pension reform. The good situation in Poland was mainly influenced by the excellent economic situation in the internal market, in which the consumer boom was maintained. In addition, the external situation and, in particular, stable growth in the euro area were good. The growth structure of demand was conducive to the implementation of the budget. The increase in household consumption was particularly high, with an increase of 4.7%. and was the highest since the outbreak of the global financial crisis in 2008. - and an increase of 8.7% imports, i.e. the category of final demand, which involved the highest tax revenue. A record-breaking increase in consumption has been the result of rising incomes and wages for decades. In 2017, after more than a quarter of a century of transformation, unemployment practically disappeared in Poland. Although the rate of registered unemployment was at the end of that year and is still 6.6%, measured according to a more economically sound BAEL methodology, the surveys on a random sample of persons have actually fallen to 4.4%. Given the significant component of the so-called. Structural unemployment - e.g. people without any professional qualifications, which are, according to the Central Statistical Office, about 1/3 of the unemployed registered - unemployment has disappeared as a phenomenon from the employer's point of view. This also means increasing difficulties in finding people willing to work, but consequently constant pressure on nominal wage growth, which increased by 5.4% in 2017 with an annual upward trend. The nominal increase in wages, also supported by other factors, including in particular the increase in social benefits, which are part of the effect of the government's policy, while at the same time a relatively small increase in prices, was the main source of rapid growth in consumption, good economics in the market and, consequently, exceptionally favourable macroeconomic conditions for the implementation of the state budget. It should be noted that Poland's economic performance in 2017 proved surprisingly good, and growth rates higher than expectations of both domestic and foreign analysts. Of course, this has been conducive to the implementation of the budget this year, especially in view of the relatively cautious macroeconomic assumptions adopted. Less than expected was the increase in investment, which amounted to 3.4%. Despite the acceleration from quarter to quarter of investment growth, gross fixed capital expenditure, the return on investment to gross domestic product decreased throughout the year. The plan of Prime Minister Mateusz Morawiecki indicates that an investment rate of 25% is needed. This is to be served by the so-called. Business Constitution - package 5 laws to make life easier for entrepreneurs. At the end of 2017, the acceleration of investment was mainly linked to greater public investment, in particular co-financed by European Union funds. On the other hand, investment by companies that have remained stagnated in previous years is crucial to the ability to maintain growth. This year's forecasts are optimistic, as shown by the indicators preceding PMI for the first half of 2018. This indicator for June was 54 points in the industrial production area. This means, according to analysts, that the growth trend in industry will extend to record 45 months in a row. Growing PMA readings above 50 points signal a rapid growth rate of economic activity. When assessing the implementation of the revenue of the State budget in 2017, it can be concluded that the revenue realised amounted to PLN 350.4 billion and was higher than the forecast of the Act of 25 billion PLN, i.e. 7.7% , and compared to revenue from 2016 it was higher by PLN 35.7 billion, i.e. over 11%. Tax revenues were higher by 15.4% than in the previous year. The clear increase in tax revenue was due to good economic situation, improved tax collection efficiency and a shift from January 2017 to December 2016. Non-tax revenue, representing 9.6% State budget revenue decreased by PLN 6.5 billion compared to last year's revenue, i.e. over 16%. They exceeded the forecast set out in the Budget Act by more than 11.2 billion, i.e. 49.8% , which was related to a higher than planned PLN 8.1 billion deposit from the profit of the National Bank of Poland and higher revenues. In 2017, the revenue from the tax on goods and services amounted to PLN 156.8 billion and was more than 13 billion higher than the amount forecasted in the Budget Act. This means the implementation of the forecast of VAT revenue contained in the Budget Act for 2017 at 109,3%. In comparison with the 2016 revenue, VAT revenues were almost 24% higher. One of the most important reasons for such positive influences was the determined fight against tax frauds. In 2017, excise revenue amounted to PLN 68.3 billion and amounted to close to 99% forecasts from the budget law. Compared to the 2016 revenue, excise tax revenues increased by PLN 2.5 billion, i.e. 3.6%. The increase in revenue in 2016 was recorded in all major items, including energy products, tobacco, alcohol and passenger cars, which resulted, among others. from the regulations implemented in 2017 and the activities of control services aimed at combating the grey zone and from increased consumer demand. In 2017, the Game Tax Revenue Act failed to fully implement the planned Game Tax revenue. The plan was made in 96%, but the revenue generated was higher than the 2016 revenue by 16.6%. The highest growth was recorded in the betting segment and is linked directly to the Act of 15 December 2016 amending the Gambling Act and certain other laws aimed at reducing the grey zone in that market. The income from personal income tax was realised in the amount of PLN 52.7 billion and was higher than planned by 3.3% and was 9.2% higher than the revenue from 2016. At the same time, corporate income tax revenues have been realised [[♪ The bell ♪]] in the amount of almost 29.8 billion, which represented 99.8% plan for 2017 and 112.8% revenue realised in 2016 In 2017...

The Supreme Audit Chamber positively assessed the implementation of the Budget Act for 2017. It also stated that the Council of Ministers' report on the implementation of the State budget for the period from 1 January to 31 December 2017 provides reliable information and data on the amount of revenue, expenditure, debts and liabilities and the result of the State budget in all material respects. Our assessments are based on 316 controls in 297 units. We have checked almost all the budget managers, the largest state-owned special-purpose funds, selected executive agencies, state legal entities, local government units and other public expenditure entities, including 123 grant beneficiaries. We also examined the correctness of the operation of the state budget by the National Bank of Poland and Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego. The detailed results of the audit are presented in separate information provided to the Marshal and to the Sejm committees. We have assessed the implementation of the state budget in individual parts and the implementation of the financial plans of the audited entities on a three-step scale. Among the 140 issues, the highest ones dominated. However, the number of positive assessments decreased from 118 in 2016 to 111 in 2017. On the other hand, the number of descriptive assessments expressed in the event of irregularities has increased. In 2016 there were 21 and in 2017 there were 26. There were also more negative assessments. In 2017, we negatively assessed the implementation of the plans of three units: the Polish Space Agency, the Fund - Central Registration of Vehicles and Drivers and the Fund for Hurted Aid and Post-Penitentiary Assistance. In 2016, the only negative assessment was the evaluation of the implementation of the financial plan of the Polish Space Agency. The opinion on the report of the Council of Ministers on the implementation of the state budget was formulated in particular on the basis of an examination of the accounts and budgetary reports. We gave a positive opinion on the correctness of the accounts for all 20 budget managers audited in this respect. We have examined accounting evidence documenting expenditure of a total amount of PLN 114 billion. We have examined the correctness of the annual reports in 103 budgetary parts and 182 third-degree directors. We gave a positive opinion on the reports in about 94 component owners in almost 90% of third-degree directors. The irregularities identified in the reports were corrected during the audit or did not have a significant impact on the correctness of the data indicated in the report of the Council of Ministers on the implementation of the State budget for the period from 1 January to 31 December 2017. The opinion expressed on the reliability of the data shall be based on objective evidence obtained during the audit studies carried out in accordance with international auditing standards. High Seym! The implementation of the Budget Act in 2017 was more favourable in macroeconomic terms than in the previous year and better than expected. The euro area countries have been developing as soon as possible since 2007 and economic growth has generally been higher in Eastern Europe than in the western part of Europe. The world's trade volume has also increased by almost 5%. , and low interest rates, moderate raw material prices and relatively stable geopolitical conditions have led investment outlays in highly developed economies to increase by 3.5% compared to their volume in 2016. Gross domestic product in Poland in 2017 increased really by 4.6% , while this increase was 3% in 2016. The main driver for economic development was private consumption, which was influenced by the increase in the number of employees and by the increase in household income caused in particular by the effects of the The development of the level of investment was also positive in 2017. While in 2016 the real gross fixed capital expenditure decreased by more than 8% compared to 2015 However, since the first quarter of 2017, the value of the investment has started to increase again. Throughout 2017 gross fixed capital expenditure was - in real terms - 3.4% higher than in 2016 The average annual rate of gold decreased both in relation to the US dollar - by 4% and to the euro - by 2.3%. The appreciation of the gold in 2017 was influenced in particular by the flow of portfolio capital to Poland. Higher import dynamics than export dynamics have reduced the positive balance of trade in goods. However, thanks to the record high balance of the exchange of services, Poland recorded a surplus in the current balance of payments account for the first time since the mid-1990s. It was 0.3%. gross domestic product. The projections of the main macroeconomic parameters adopted for the draft budget law for 2017 have proved to be underestimated. The state budget, the budget of the European funds and the financial plans of the non-budget units of the public finance sector have been implemented in accordance with the budget law. The total revenue of the state budget and the European budget amounted to PLN 398 billion in 2017 and was higher than that of PLN 44.3 billion in the previous year. State budget revenue amounted to 350.4 billion in 2017 and amounted to 88% Total revenue. They were almost 36 billion higher than the revenues realised in the previous year and almost 25 billion higher than the forecast. They made it possible to finance 93.3% expenditure, while in the two previous years this figure was 87%. The highest share of state budget revenue, reaching 90% They had tax revenue. This share increased by 3.2 percentage points compared to the previous year. This was at the expense of a reduction in the share of non-tax income. Tax revenue amounted to PLN 315.3 billion and was more than 14 billion higher than the forecast in the Budget Act. For this difference in 94% high implementation of revenue from the tax on goods and services, which was 13.3 billion PLN higher than laid down in the Budget Act, has been achieved. The revenue from other main tax sources was close to the forecast. The increase in tax revenue compared to 2016 was due to a good economic situation, improved tax collection efficiency and a shift in taxes on goods and services from the first half of 2017 to December 2016. The Minister for Development and Finance recognized the risks and consequences fairly, initiated and implemented legislative and organisational changes aimed at combating fraud against goods and services. A wide range of changes have been made in sealing the collection of this and other taxes. Among them were: the so-called. VAT sealing package, criminal regulation to combat VAT fraud, the obligation to transfer tax data in the form of a single control file, fuel and transport packages, solutions to reduce irregularities in gambling and to reduce from 15 000. Euros up to 15 000 zł limit value of transactions between traders executed without a payment account. In a gradual manner, IT tools were introduced to enable tax administrations to verify more efficiently both at central and local levels the data provided by taxpayers in purchase and sale records and VAT returns. Since 1 March 2017, the National Tax Administration has been operating, which has been opened inter alia. to increase the efficiency of cooperation between tax administrations, improve the flow of information, improve the effectiveness of the fight against tax fraud and the recovery of tax and customs arrears. A comprehensive assessment of the effects of the new tax administration structure will, of course, be possible after a long period of time. At present, it is difficult to make clear to what extent the increase in tax revenues was the result of sealing and organisational measures and to what extent the result of economic recovery. Guarantees and economic guarantees in portfolio mode for a total amount of approx. PLN 10.5 billion, including nearly 40 thousand. de minimis guarantees for the total amount of 9.9 billion PLN. The very good performance of Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego in supporting the activities of small and medium-sized enterprises through the provision of de minimis guarantees shows that this offer is well suited to the expectations of both small and medium-sized enterprises and the banking sector. It provides financing for the day-to-day activities of small and medium-sized enterprises as well as investment projects carried out by them. The success of this programme is due in particular to the fact that it is nationwide and operates on a country-wide standard of product, which due to its parameters is very easy to apply by banks despite differences in their credit offer. The procedure for granting portfolio guarantees is very simplified as the trader arranges all the formalities for obtaining the guarantee in the bank in which he takes credit. In addition, in 2017 a thousand guarantees of student loans were also granted for PLN 19.7 million. In the light of the information data, it should be noted that the guarantees and guarantees provided by the State Treasury and the Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego under government programmes do not pose a risk to public finances, but at the same time constitute an important instrument to support economic processes, mainly in the field of road and rail infrastructure, as well as the activities of small and medium-sized enterprises. During today's speech, I would like to focus only on the most important actions taken by the bank in 2017. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! By virtue of the article. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation in accordance with the examination procedure referred to in paragraph 1 of this Article. 1 The Constitution of the Republic of Poland has the exclusive right to determine and implement monetary policy. According to the constitution, NBP is responsible for the value of Polish money. Art. 3 The NBP Act clarifies the tasks set out in the Constitution, stating: "The basic purpose of NBP's activities is to maintain a stable level of prices while supporting the economic policy of the Government, unless this limits the basic objective of NBP. In order to maintain price stability, the NBP pursues the inflation target strategy used by the main central banks in the world. Its essence is to anchor inflation expectations at the level of the announced inflation target and adapt monetary policy instruments, mainly interest rates, to the state and outlook of the economy and current and expected price dynamics. Since 2004, the NBP's medium-term inflation target has been 2,5% since 2004 with a symmetrical deviation range of plus/minus 1 percentage point. In 2017, the average annual inflation rate after several years of remaining below the NBP inflation target developed close to it and stood at 2% compared to 0.6% in 2016, so that we are very close to the monetary inflation target set by the Monetary Policy Council. The increase in inflation in 2017 was driven by a higher dynamic of food prices, especially domestic prices, as well as energy due to an increase in oil prices on world markets. Base inflation, on the other hand, remained low, supported by limited domestic demand pressure, low inflation abroad, and the strengthening of the gold exchange rate. Moreover, despite falling unemployment and accelerating wage dynamics, the labour market did not generate excessive inflationary pressure in 2017. As a result of the increase in labour productivity, the dynamics of unit labour costs remained moderate. In 2017, the economic situation in Poland was favourable. Growth accelerated to 4.6% from 3.0% in 2016 The main growth factor remained consumer demand, supported by a systematic increase in employment and accelerated wage growth. The payout of benefits, including under the scheme "Family of 500+', also had an impact on this. Investment has also been revived. GDP growth in Poland was further encouraged by a relatively high increase in activity in the Polish economy, mainly in the euro area, which is Poland's main trading partner. In 2017 there were no signs of any macroeconomic imbalances in the Polish economy. In foreign trade Poland remained surplus, thanks to which the current account balance was positive. At the same time, the dynamics of credit for the non-financial sector were close to the dynamics of nominal GDP growth. This year too, inflation is moderate and economic growth is similar to that observed in the second half of the previous year. The outlook for the next quarter is favourable and inflation forecasts indicate that it will remain close to the inflation target. In view of the above considerations, last year NBP maintained interest rates unchanged, including a reference rate of 1,5%. The NBP's stability of interest rates ensured low inflation and supported sustainable economic growth, including the recovery of investment and the stability of the financial system. The NBP therefore continues to pursue its core objective of ensuring price stability and supporting the government's economic policy. Mrs. Marshal! Mr. Messengers! Gentlemen! Monetary policy was implemented by the NBP in 2017 by means of a set of instruments adopted by the Monetary Policy Council. The conditions on domestic and foreign financial markets did not require NBP to use instruments to a different extent than in the previous year. The main monetary policy instrument in 2017 was the NBP interest rates. The NBP reference rate was the most important. This rate, determining the profitability of the open market operations used by the central bank to shape liquidity conditions in the banking sector, affected the level of interest rate on the funds made available in the in-bank market. In turn, the NBP's deposit and pawning rates set a range of overnight fluctuations on this market. The NBP implemented monetary policy in 2017 under conditions of persistent overflow of the banking sector. Its level was on average PLN 73.6 billion in the year and was close to that of the previous year. In 2017, the operational objective of monetary policy was to establish the Polonia rate near the NBP reference rate. This objective has been achieved effectively. The average absolute deviation of the Polonia rate from the NBP reference rate was 12 percentage points compared to 16 percentage points in 2016. The main tool used to achieve the operational objective of monetary policy was open market operations carried out in the form of issuance of NBP cash vouchers. Their issue was intended to absorb excess funds remaining on the accounts of the Bank in the Central Bank and to maintain balanced liquidity conditions in the banking sector. The implementation of the operational objective in 2017 supported NBP's deposit and credit operations. By offering them, the central bank allowed banks to locate surplus funds or to supplement their shortfalls for 1-day periods by using a deposit at the end of the day and a pawn shop loan accordingly. As in previous years, the minimum reserve system played an important role in achieving the operational objective of monetary policy. In view of its average nature, it gave entities subject to a reserve requirement in the NBP considerable discretion to decide on the amount of funds collected in central bank accounts during the reserve period. As a result, reducing the pressure resulting from changes in liquidity conditions, the minimum reserve system encouraged the stabilisation of market interest rates. In 2017, the Monetary Policy Council introduced two changes to the minimum reserve rate. In accordance with the Decision of 8 November 2017, the reserve ratio of at least 2 years was set at 0%. , starting with the minimum reserve held since 30 April 2018. The Council's decision resulted in the codification of this parameter of the reserve system in respect of resources obtained from both domestic and foreign entities, which had not been done before. In turn, according to the Decision of 5 December 2017, it was reduced to 0,5%. the interest rate on the reserve assets. This amendment entered into force on 1 January 2018. The changes made to the minimum reserve system should help to reduce the mismatch of the fixed-term structure of the assets and liabilities of Polish institutions subject to reserve requirements by supporting long-term savings. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The second area of the core activity of the National Bank of Poland was the stabilisation policy, the stabilisation of the banking system and the financial system of Poland. This is a prerequisite for ensuring sustainable economic development and achieving the core objective of NBP, i.e. maintaining a stable price level. The NBP's statutory tasks are to promote the stability of the financial system and to eliminate or reduce systemic risk. The NBP carries out this task by conducting cyclical research and analysis on the stability and development of the financial system and cooperation with other state bodies, including within the Financial Stability Committee. We are taking various types of measures that promote the security of the financial system and are also involved in the creation of national and European financial regulations. Maintaining the security of the banking sector is of course essential to maintaining the stability of the financial system in Poland. The assets of this system represent more than 2/3 of the assets of national financial institutions. In turn, the non-credit financial institutions sector, due to its financial situation and the business models used, does not pose significant risks to financial stability. Analysis of the financial system and its economic environment in order to identify the main risks to financial stability in Poland showed that last year the Polish financial system was stable. The credit dynamics for the non-financial sector in 2017 after taking into account the exchange rate effects amounted to 6,1% and was close to the dynamics of nominal GDP growth. Such a rate of credit was not a source of imbalances in the economy and in the financial system, nor was it a barrier to economic growth. There were no indications of excessive mitigation of banks' credit policies and the quality of the bank's credit portfolio was stable. Banks were exposed to very limited market risks and liquidity and financing risks. The capital equipment of the banking sector was appropriate to the scale and nature of the business. Most banks complied with regulatory requirements, including the maintenance of additional capital buffers. The total capital ratio in the banking sector, excluding BGK and branches of credit institutions, reached 18.1% at the end of 2017 , against 17,2% at the end of 2016 and a minimum regulatory level of 8%. The increase in this ratio was mainly the result of bank retention of profits made during previous periods. The persistence of uncertainty in the external environment of Poland indicates the need to maintain high resilience of the banking sector in Poland. In this context, it is a positive phenomenon to put an end to the downward trend in the profitability of banks, which could limit their ability to supplement capital in the future. ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-04-sejm-66-2.u192	Dear Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen! Information on the implementation of the Act on Special Economic Zones is a document prepared annually in connection with the provision contained in the said Act, which obliges the Council of Ministers to provide the Sejm with information on the implementation of this Act together with a report on the implementation of the budget. The information shall include data on the functioning of special economic zones as at 31 December 2017 and shall cover issues such as the location and level of management of each of the 14 zones; the effects of the zones, including the number of permits granted, the jobs created, the value and the industrial structure of the investments carried out and the country of origin of the capital; the activities of the zones management companies. In 2017, the boundaries of nine special economic zones changed. The total area of the zones has increased by more than 1.2 thousand. ha and currently stands at 22.7 thousand. ha. The average level of zoning was around 60%. - from 25.6% in the Legnica zone up to 76.8% in the Tarnobrzeg zone. As of 31 December 2017, entrepreneurs had 2349 valid business permits in special economic zones. The most active permits are in the Katowice zone - 329 and the least in the Kamienna Góra zone - 60. In 2017, 346 permits were issued, i.e. 34 more than in 2016. The accumulated value of invested capital since the beginning of the zones amounted to nearly PLN 106.6 billion. It's a drop of almost 5.7 billion, i.e. 5% compared to the end of the previous year. The decrease was the result of the expiry of 202 authorisations issued in 10 zones. In 2017, the largest share in the cumulative value of the investment was: Wałbrzyska - 21.38%, Katowice - 15.58% And the boat - 12.5%. Total employment in all zones as at 31 December 2017 amounted to over 353 thousand. persons. That's $21,000. persons, i.e. 6.3%, more than the previous year. The structure of the investment by country of origin of the capital is as follows: Polish companies - almost 21.5% , foreign companies - more than 78.5% , of which Germany - 18.8% , Netherlands - 8,2%, United States - 8,1%. The industry structure has not changed compared to the previous year. The largest share in the cumulative value of the investment was by companies from the automotive industry - 24% Volkswagen Motor Poland, Volkswagen Poznan in Września, BASF Poland, Toyota, Opel Manufacturing Poland, Brembo Poland; rubber and plastic products - 9,9% , including the largest investors in this industry are Michelin Poland, Kronospan HPL, Dębica; metal products - 7,7% Bison Chucks, Porta KMI Poland, KAN, Servist, Bilfinger Mars Offshore, Ball Packaging Europe Lublin. According to data provided by the Ministry of Finance, the total amount of public aid granted to enterprises operating in zones by the end of 2016 amounted to PLN 22.5 billion. This amount represents 20% of the investment made by entrepreneurs by the end of 2016. The total value of investments incurred by investors in special economic zones amounted to PLN 112.2 billion. Investments of companies managing zones in infrastructure amounted to PLN 42.2 million, which represents 15.9% total expenditure on infrastructure incurred in 2017. This amount was lower by 74,6 million than in the previous year. For the promotion of zones in 2017, management companies allocated over PLN 7.07 million, i.e. by PLN 1.4 million more than in 2016. As at 31 December 2017, none of the management companies recorded a negative financial result. The highest financial result achieved: Kostrzyn-Słubicka Special Economic Zone SA - 22,98 Mioł zł, Agencja Rozwoju Przemysłu SA Branch in Tarnobrzeg - 18.2 mln PLN, Katowice Special Economic Zone SA - 17.63 million PLN. The total amount of tax exemption of companies managing zones from the beginning of the zones until the end of 2017 amounted to almost PLN 260 million. In 2017, the total amount of the tax exemption amounted to PLN 17.6 million and was lower by PLN 0.7 million compared to the previous year. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The 12th Radio and TV Act. I am pleased to present a report for 2017. Ladies and gentlemen, 2017 was quite an important year for the Polish audiovisual market. There were major changes both within television broadcasters and within radio broadcasters. I would like to remind you that the purchase of the mother company, Scripps Networks, by Discovery is a global transaction involving the participation of these companies worldwide. But in Poland this had a special dimension due to the market position of TVN programmes. As far as these and other processes are concerned, they are relevant from the point of view of one of the statutory responsibilities of the National Radio and Television Council, i.e. the tasks of protecting pluralism in the media, due to the convergence process, please countries, and the transformation of the media market. The threats to pluralism are noticeable all over the world. This is, ladies and gentlemen, a rather serious problem. I don't think I'd take that as a reason for mockery. The recommendations of the Council of Europe Committee of Ministers of 7 March this year are worth mentioning. Other institutions also take the convergence and concentration process very seriously, above all, of the media. Convergence and concentration have been forced by new technologies, new distribution channels, also a new way of consumption of the media and consolidation processes that take place worldwide. This carries signs of the threat of pluralism through globalization processes. That's what happens all over the world. The Committee of Ministers of the Council of Europe pointed out the need to introduce appropriate legislation on media ownership, including on the control of media ownership and funding, supporting the transparency of ownership, public access to information and databases showing the structure of media ownership. At the same time, he stressed the importance of protecting pluralism in the media, the diversity of content and institutional framework of pluralism, as well as the role of public media and local and environmental media. This part concerning the concentration of market mechanisms, changes in the Polish media market is very widely presented in the information of the National Radio and Television Council for 2017. I mention this because it is quite important, it is about fulfilling the statutory obligation, which was imposed on the National Council of Radio and Television. In addition, we carried out activities on a number of other sections and fields, including. further development of digital terrestrial television and digital terrestrial broadcasting is taking place. The National Radio and Television Council is preparing to announce digital frequencies in 34 cities. We want local digital radio multiplexes to be created there. I remind you that these are large cities such as Kraków, Gdańsk, Warsaw, Szczecin and Poznań, but also smaller cities such as Łomża, Koszalin and Siedlce. It's 34 cities, which is a very large population coverage. We hope that this will be a great support for the development of local radio, because this process, which has been going in the right direction for many years, namely the consolidation of radio broadcasters and in fact the fall - can also be used this quite dramatic formulation - local radio stations. At the beginning of the trial there were several hundred, now there are only a few dozen, and those really independent - even less. This is therefore an important challenge for the State, including for the regulator of the market, which is the National Radio and Television Council. So we want to announce these frequencies. At the same time, Polish Radio continues broadcasting in DAB+, and the other two multiplexes prepared for national radio stations, i.e. trans-regional radio stations, remain undeveloped and are waiting for an offer from current broadcasters who broadcast in analogue technology. We do not postulate what was an important signal for the market in 2017, the exemption or we do not specify the date of the exemption, so-called. Switch-off, analogue frequency. This was the basis for a rather profound dispute between the previous regulator, the previous composition of the national council and radio broadcasters. We do not believe that there is a need for a statutory order to disable analog radio or a statutory order to conduct so-called. simulcast, i.e. parallel analogue and digital broadcasting. On the other hand, we informed the broadcasters, we informed them that these frequencies are, of course, intended for their digital offer, but at a time when the digital radio will be sufficiently developed, they will simply no longer be reserved and will be issued in the tendering procedure. A concessional one, not a bargaining one. As far as digital terrestrial television is concerned, a communication on the future of the free-of-charge television market is important because it concerns this issue. We have identified that the issue of multiplex 5, which is still a multiplex to be used, undeveloped, will be announced as soon as possible, but no earlier than 30 June 2020. Until then, Poland is obliged to release the 700 MHz band for mobile broadband systems. This involves planning frequencies for digital terrestrial television, and therefore this multiplex according to the position of the Electronic Communications Office will be possible to run after the date of full refarming of these frequencies, and the release of the 700 MHz band. Therefore, we will then announce concession proceedings concerning this multiplex. We have announced that we will want to introduce this in the new technology, DVB-T2 terrestrial digital television technology with HEVC encoding. In short, it comes down to the fact that we will offer high-resolution television as a standard. We will gradually also want to move on to other multiplexes with this technology, so we are counting on the relevant regulations of the Ministry of Digitalization, which will clarify and define this, but we want to gradually move on to better, more efficient and better quality technology after 2020, which involves the adaptation of consumer devices, i.e. the exchange of televisions, or the installation of suitable adapters, which is happening anyway, i.e. The new TVs are already being implemented today. For the market this is important information, in which we have made another message that we do not foresee a significant extension of the free television offer on terrestrial television. About the current DVB-T multiplex capacity will correspond to DVB-T2 multiplex capacity in high resolution technology. This is important for broadcasters who must predict with what competition They're gonna face it in the future. Last year we started working on the implementation of this project, this year we already have the money in the budget for it, and next year we will continue to use it and we will organise these studies. What is important, we want to invite to this study all market players who use this research and work with their use, i.e. media homes, advertisers, marketers, and broadcasters themselves. The public media and PAP systems in place at the end of 2015 were characterised by far-reaching shortcomings that had to be eliminated in order to ensure their implementation of the mission. One of the first steps of the government to be able to implement measures to improve the situation in public media was the establishment of a National Media Council. In 2017, the National Media Council held 17 meetings. At and outside the meetings, the National Media Council adopted 75 resolutions on matters falling within its area of activity. In order to ensure the functioning of Polish Radio, the Council continued its competition for the position of President of Polish Radio SA in 2016, which resulted in its appointment for a four-year term. During this year the Council also dealt with the situation of the Polish Press Agency. In October 2017, the National Media Council decided that the new board would be double. The result of the National Media Council's activities was the establishment of a new board already in early 2018. As regards the formation of supervisory boards, the National Media Council, instead of the seven-member Supervisory Board, set up a new, much less numerous, because the three-member Supervisory Board. On the Polish Television, a new member was appointed as chairman of the Supervisory Board of TVP after having resigned from the Board of the previous President. In connection with the expiry of the current programme councils, the National Media Council has set up 37 new programme councils in public broadcasting companies, TVP, TVP Polonia and the Polish Press Agency. The Council also exercised other powers arising from the provisions of the Act, inter alia. agreed to change the statutes, initiated amendments to the statutes, agreed to appoint directors of the regional branches of TVP, defined the minimum share of broadcasts created by the field units of TVP in national programmes in 2018. On several occasions, the Council took a stand on matters important for the functioning of public media. Special attention should be paid here, among others.: to appeal for the non-employment of former officials, employees and collaborators of the special services of the Polish People's Republic, to appeal for respect for journalistic independence and to refrain from using the language and actions of aggression, a position on the negative effects on public companies of radiophony and television resulting from the amendment of the VAT Act. In order to obtain detailed information on the matter of broadcasters, the National Media Council met with representatives of public broadcasting companies, particularly TVP SA representatives. By safeguarding the wider public interest, members of the Council also met with representatives of different backgrounds. Following all events known in December 2016, the Council met with representatives of Emitel in order to explain the causes of multiple disturbances of TVP signal. It is also worth mentioning here a meeting with representatives of the Advertising Council. In the framework of a broad discussion, among other things, there was a discussion. about advertising properties of dietary supplements, pharmaceuticals, cosmetics and additives. Ladies and gentlemen! Legal status prior to the entry into force of the Act on the National Media Council did not favour cooperation between individual public broadcasting companies and PAP SA. This has often resulted in a waste of energy and resources for repeating various initiatives, and even cooperation with commercial media, which competed on the market for public media. It is worth pointing out once again that, for the first time in the history of the National Media Council, two seats in the five-person opposition were legally guaranteed, which ensures pluralism and guarantees access to information about the activities of companies. In 2017, the National Radio and Television Council exercised its competence during 55 meetings during which it adopted resolutions in the field of its activities. During the reporting period, 24 concession competitions were decided by the National Radio and Television Council. Radio broadcasters have received three new concessions for the dissemination of local programmes. As part of the so-called process. The National Council adopted 10 decisions on the renewal of radio and television programmes. In addition, 25 licences were issued for the dissemination of programmes on cable networks. In total, 244 concession decisions were issued during the reporting period and 15 decision to impose financial penalties on broadcasters for a total amount of 274 000. zł. During the reporting period, within the reporting powers, the Board worked on issuing six regulations implementing laws, four of which were published in the Official Journal of the Laws. The work on the other two continued in 2018. The National Radio and Television Council distributed over PLN 1 billion to carry out missionary tasks by public broadcasters, including PLN 697 million from licence receipts collected by the Polish Post, and the remainder for compensation of revenues lost in 2010 and 2017 due to statutory redundancies of certain groups of subscribers. The distribution of these funds is as follows: Polish Television received almost PLN 621 million, Polish Radio - more than 193 million, and 17 regional radio stations of Polish Radio - more than 188 935 thousand. zł. These amounts were transferred to carry out the tasks agreed with the National Radio and Television Council, contained in the 2017 financial and programme plans. The National Radio and Television Council, like the National Media Council, held positions on key issues for the functioning of public media. In her statement of 23 February, she objected to the content promoted in the TV programme of Superstation, in which there were violent and hateful statements directed at journalists working in the company Television Polska SA. At the end of the proceedings, the President of the National Radio and Television Council issued a decision to punish the sender. In its statement of 14 December 2017, the Council critically referred to all manifestations of a rapidly increasing wave of verbal and physical aggression directed against journalists. The revenue generated by the National Radio and Television Council in 2017 amounted to more than PLN 24 million. They consisted mainly of revenues from concession fees and penalties imposed on broadcasters. The operating costs amounted to over 24.6 million and were higher than the budget revenue by PLN 0.5 million. High Izbo! [[Applause]] We proceed with the examination of item 21 of the agenda: Report from the Committee on Infrastructure and the Committee on Territorial Government and Regional Policy on the Government's draft law on State aid in the payment of housing expenses in the first years of renting the apartment (prints Nos 2651 and 2664). I ask Mr Bogdan Rzońca to submit a report to the committee.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-04-sejm-66-2.u302	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! High Seym! The first reading of the Government's draft law on State aid in the payment of housing expenses in the first years of renting the apartment, print No. 2651, took place on 27 June 2018 at the meeting of the Sejm. On 27 June and 2 July 2018, the Joint Committees: Infrastructure and Local Government and Regional Policy appointed for the substantive examination of the draft law worked over the draft law article after the article. The legal solution consisting of financial assistance in the form of rent subsidies pursues two main objectives: the social objective - by addressing income subsidies for people who are able to purchase housing on their own, with the right standard on the current housing market, taking into account the intended pro-familyness of solutions that show preference for more than one household member; the investment objective - by addressing subsidies for people who settle new buildings, whose implementation increases the volume of housing investments. In addition, the rent subsidies strengthen the local dimension of housing policy by the leading role of municipal authorities in the selection of investors and the establishment of the rules for the qualification of tenants. Aid may also be part of a policy encouraging investment in areas undergoing regeneration. The rent payments are addressed to households with a certain level of income and without any other apartment. The rent payments will be granted for the period indicated in the project and will depend on the local cost of building the apartments, the occupied area of the apartment and the number of household members. An annual verification of the right to aid and the amount of aid is foreseen in terms of compliance with the criteria, as well as the conduct by the municipalities of the selection of tenants taking into account the criteria set by the municipality's board and the conclusion of lease agreements with tenants after examination of the ability to pay the rent on time. The aid will be applied for first-time housing. These may be newly built apartments or apartments located in existing buildings located in the area of a special revitalization zone, undergoing renovation or reconstruction. Such a solution is intended to create new housing investments for rentals that would fill this identified market gap. It is assumed that the result of the programme will be the creation of a housing sector for rent built by institutional entities managing entire buildings with apartments for rent. It is possible that, thanks to the payments, rents in these apartments will also be available to people with average income for whom there is currently no adequate market offer. It was proposed that the Act should enter into force on 1 January 2019 with the exception of certain provisions which should enter into force after 14 days from the date of publication. Several substantive amendments were adopted to the draft law. In addition, legislative and editorial amendments were tabled by the Legislative Bureau to clarify some of the proposed provisions. The two joint committees have completed their work and recommend a further legislative process in this area. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-04-sejm-66-2.u303	Thank you very much. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. First on behalf of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, Mr Bogdan Rzońc.

Each month of this year, when there are no subsidies under the scheme, more than 15 thousand are sold. apartments. Without stimulating demand, we have a situation in which this market is growing. ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-04-sejm-66-2.u350	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Dear Minister, On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present my position on the report of the Committee on Public Finance, print No 2700, draft law amending the Tax Order and some other laws, initial printing No 2626. The amendments to this Act include provisions on blocking in the account of the eligible entity. In order to improve the efficiency of the split payment system, the bill introduces the possibility of using the STIR channel, i.e. the IT system of the clearing house, to send information by the head of the tax office to banks or SKOKs about the decision to release funds from the VAT account. Changes to ensure greater completeness and legibility of the so-called Black letter will support entrepreneurs in the proper performance of tax obligations, among others. through the ability to verify the integrity of their counterparties. During the committee's work, it was confirmed that the right to challenge the blocking of the eligible entity's account for a period of up to 72 hours and to challenge the limitation of the scope of that blockade was abolished. This is the right solution because at that time the tax authorities will be able to coordinate with other services, including in particular the prosecutor. The obligation to provide information on IP addresses is introduced so that the risk indicator and the risk analysis carried out by the Head of National Tax Administration can be more precise. Data that facilitate the identification and residence of persons actually using a blocked account are necessary to establish networks of interconnections and relationships of persons actually using accounts that are usually organisers or participants in tax fraud. In the case of fiscal offences, sham economic activity is registered with other persons, so-called. poles which have no assets and often have no knowledge of the real purpose of the project in which they participate. During our work in committee, we changed the way banks provide information about IP addresses and related information. The proposed amendment takes account of the demand of the banking environment. We have also implemented the deadlines for implementing the amendments introduced by the Tax Order. We have also introduced an amendment that will allow the head of the KAS to issue a provision for the transfer of funds from the blocked account of the eligible entity to any maintenance pension, not just a maintenance pension which is deemed to be compensation. The proposed changes are intended to further seal the tax system. The amendments introduced in the Committee on Public Finance for many hours, including a dozen legislative amendments, improve the original bill. On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I support the report of the Committee on Public Finance, print no.2700, draft law amending the Tax Order Act and some other laws. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-04-sejm-66-2.u354	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! The draft law includes provisions on the blocking of eligible entities' accounts. It introduces far-reaching constraints, especially for entrepreneurs, and at the same time provides for new, extensive responsibilities for financial institutions, banks and SKOKs. The definition of the eligible entity's account has been extended, including the fixed-term deposit account and the VAT account resulting from the Act introducing the split payment mechanism. The most important element of the project is the abolition of the possibility to challenge the blocking of the eligible entity's account for a period of 72 hours and to challenge the limitation of the scope of that blockade. Only a provision to extend the period of blockade for a period of up to 3 months may be challenged. The examination of this project at the meeting of the Committee on Public Finance did not bring about significant substantive changes. The votes of the opposition and the request not to analyse or debate this project without the presence of the appointed spokesperson for small and medium-sized enterprises in this discussion, as well as a representative of the Minister of Finance in the rank of Secretary or Undersecretary of State. ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-04-sejm-66-2.u364	Thank you. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I wanted to apologize again for my partial absence from the committee meeting last night. This was due, as I said, to reasons entirely independent of me, related to the responsibilities of the Financial Supervision Authority. Ladies and gentlemen! The first question is whether there was a comment on the rejected amendment which appeared at the committee meeting. This is not the result of legislative negligence or any mistake in the bill. It is about introducing an additional possibility to use this system, which was created in the National Chamber of Settlements, for the purposes of the banking system, so that the infrastructure created works not only to combat tax fraud or tax offences, but also in other related areas such as. money laundering or bank fraud. In this respect, this amendment must be assessed by the government from the point of view of the extent to which these additional analyses would concern whether the KIR could analyse data collected from banks or publicly available data, as well as the time at which such possibility should be available to the clearing house and indirectly to banks in the context of progress in the fight against VAT fraud. It is important that these functionalities do not cause collisions and that we have a clear path of action, where the priority is, of course, to counter VAT fraud. So this is not a legislative problem or a problem with the need for another amendment, it is a decision that we believe requires government procedure. A question about a small taxpayer. I understand that you are concerned about the liquidity of small taxpayers. This, of course, is also the subject of our concern. This system is to be used as an exception to the exception and it is a tool, it can be said, in a sense, definitive in a situation where the system reports a number of subsequent financial flows which are symptomatic for the flow of these funds abroad. It is at this point, just before the money flows abroad and the loss of control over it, that a lock-in instrument can finally be applied, so here really there is no risk for a small taxpayer, here is the chance to clear our economic space from these pseudo-taxpayers, pseudo-entrepreneurs, who had previously put a small average taxpayer at risk of getting involved in the VAT carousel. And this unfortunately has been happening for many years, and this small average taxpayer unfortunately today, having fiscal, criminal and treasure proceedings, has a problem due to the State's passivity in this area in previous years. Will 72 hours be enough to assess the taxpayer or the accounts for the risk of tax fraud? They'll have to do. This kind of timespace is provided for in various other laws, such as money laundering. There is a little bit more time there, but thanks to the new analytical possibilities, thanks to the data from the JPK submitted by taxpayers every month, we have much better analytical possibilities, from the point of view of their duration. On the one hand, these 72 hours must suffice, and on the other hand, placing these locks will be much more precise due to the data range and the quality of the analyses. A question about the multiple accounts with the taxpayer. On the one hand, the STIR system allows us to see every day all the accounts of entrepreneurs, the savings and settlement accounts of entrepreneurs, how they change, how they are assumed, how they are eliminated, we see it during the day, so it is a significant improvement in the awareness of the tax authorities in relation to what was before, and this is a certain advantage of this system - the rate of transmission and quality of these accounts data, on the other hand I would like to say that soon there will be a White Letter Act, which also - like the Czech Republic, which many years ago introduced a system of disclosure of all business accounts - improves the safety of business turnover, the security of accounts, when we all just know who is using the account, and there are no understatements of the type of transfers to just established account outside the Treasury radar. It's a constitutional question. I would like to say that these solutions may not even be modelled as much as they are calculators for anti-money laundering solutions. And this is a profiled carbon, i.e. dedicated to this problem of VAT fraud, tax fraud. I would like to remind you that anti-money laundering solutions are modelled on a directive, they have never been challenged from a constitutional point of view, although they have been in force for many, many years, and these mechanisms - there is no appeal from the blockade - they have never been challenged from a constitutional point of view, and they also fulfil their role in clearing financial space from such harmful transactions, transfers, flows. Question about the legal form of the head of the KAS. This is an unnamed legal act, but it is subject to reporting to a prosecutor who has an immediate insight into this situation and an immediate opportunity to assess the correctness of that decision, its protective action. Of course, the real liquidity loss of the entrepreneur by extending the rapid blockade for the next 3 months is subject to full judicial protection, through a judicial assessment of this administrative action. Of course, a blockage for 3 months is the element that should be in accordance with this law - and is - judged by the courts, because it actually leads to real liquidity damage. One more comment about the blockade process itself - thanks to this system, thanks to the knowledge of the number of bank accounts held by the taxpayer concerned, we can lock all accounts that are registered with the entity in question. This means that such action is in a sense much more effective than in the case of single queries as to whether a taxpayer has a bank account, which unfortunately has been a problem for many years in the past. This system allows for precise and effective prevention of money removal from the Polish financial system. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-04-sejm-66-2.u372	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! It was my honor as a representative of the Law and Justice Club to present to the High House an opinion on the draft Public-Private Partnership Act, print No 2333. This draft consists of proposals for 13 laws from different legal fields. As the name suggests, it is the Public-Private Partnership Act. This is a very important, responsible bill because it allows for close cooperation of private companies with public and public sector entities at business level. So far, despite the fact that the law already in force has been beneficial for all, the application of its records has been very unpopular. This law lays down rules so that both the official and the entrepreneur have a clear and secure legal framework in which they can and should move without the risk of conflict with the law. The main objective of this law is to enable contracts to be concluded consisting in the division of responsibilities and the financial burden of the public sector to the benefit of a private entity, but not to exempt it from liability and supervision. In the case of such an agreement, the public sector makes investments without financial investment or with a very small symbolic effort, and the whole financial burden is taken over by the investor. In a word, this is the allocation of economic competence to a private entity, i.e. he makes investments from his own funds, and the investor, the public sector, takes over only administrative competence, i.e. supervising. Let me give you an example: building a multi-storey parking lot with private funds later using it for 30 years, which gave the investor a return on the costs incurred. This law is very necessary and beneficial, both for the state, public and economic sectors, because thanks to this law the public sector has a chance to so-called. A quick leap to the future, because it can implement both these smaller and multi-billion-dollar investments without engaging its own resources. This gives a chance for a very large development, even an explosion of investment, because the private sector has funds that are willing to invest under rational conditions, especially since - as we know - many municipalities are already on the verge of statutory debt and will probably be forced to use this law. This law is also an opportunity for the Polish economy and Polish companies, because it opens new investment horizons to them, which consequently stimulates the economy, eliminates unemployment and, what is important, because they are mostly long-term investments, they create such a company so-called. positive economic stabilisation ballast. The basic objectives of the Act are: to relieve the budgets of public entities in the investment phase by providing financing for private entities; to ensure better quality of services and the infrastructure provided, as we know, it will be in the interest of the contractor to make quality optimal, because he will be its administrator, i.e. the user for many years; to implement innovative solutions for the construction, maintenance or provision of services and can be implemented in different ways; to define and develop friendly rules for the use of the public-private partnership formula; to raise public-private partnership awareness as an equivalent alternative method for providing high-quality public infrastructure and services. High Izbo! The Law and Justice Club welcomes this law and asks the High House to accept and adopt this law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

We also propose to modify the article. Article 7a (7) (a) (ii) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 is replaced by the following: 4, which more clearly defines the liability of the entity. In the next amendment, we say that the private partner shall report to the public at least every three months on the implementation of the project. The detailed reporting procedure is, of course, defined by the PPP agreement. We would also like the Minister responsible for regional development to report on the implementation of PPP tasks not only to the Council of Ministers but also to the Parliamentary Committee. That's what happens, for example. in the case of economic zones. I think it is not a great burden for the Committee of the Economy to receive such reports. We propose, in the last amendment, the deletion of the article. 16. The distinction of PPPs in the CBA law in our reasoning is really wrong, unnecessary and creates a bad atmosphere that we now want to avoid. We want to encourage and create a bad atmosphere around this kind of activity. Let us ask ourselves whether these proposed provisions are sufficient to increase interest. This debated project is updating this law, as well as 19 other laws. In general, it is intended to improve the legal environment of public-private partnerships and remove the shortcomings that limit the emergence of PPPs. The PPP formula is quite widespread in many countries, unfortunately it has not yet worked in our Polish conditions. In 10 years only 116 entities operating on the basis of public-private partnership have been established in Poland. The proposed law aims to introduce legal solutions that will enable the efficient preparation and better implementation of infrastructure projects in the PPP formula. The aim is to increase the degree of private capital involvement in the implementation of public investment and services in Poland. One of the most important changes made is that any investment made by the State budget of more than PLN 300 million, which is not financed by Union funds, will be mandatory at an early stage of the preparatory work, is analysed as to its feasibility in the PPP formula. That's so-called. PPP test. This analysis will then be the subject of the opinion of the Minister for Regional Development. In addition, any public entity will be able to ask the Minister for Regional Development for an opinion on the planned project, regardless of the size of the planned investment. It's so-called. certification. The project introduces an obligation to analyse the effectiveness of the implementation of the project in the PPP formula and introduces rules to facilitate monitoring of this market and to enable projects to be carried out on the basis of existing public entity companies. The project allows local authorities to grant special grants to private partners to finance investments related to the implementation of local tasks. At present, such grants are only admissible for non-profit-making entities. The project provides for a limit on the expenditure of the state budget related to the financial performance of the Act of less than PLN 17 million, and the government assumes that by 2020 100 new contracts will be signed in the form of a public-private partnership. The project provides and describes in very detail, is quite extensive, conduct and activities conducive to the emergence of actors in the public-private partnership formula. You'd want them to be effective and functional. It would be very good if such projects would appear more courageously on the Polish market. My club, the Members' Club of the Polish People's Party - the Union of European Democrats, will support this project. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-04-sejm-66-2.u392	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I wanted to say that the Ministry of Investment and Development, and earlier the Ministry of Development, has been running a wide promotional campaign for over a year, a training campaign. To date, some 1600 people have been trained, they are largely self-government, but there have also been special trainings dedicated to control authorities, including one CBA-dedicated training - CBA employees have been trained to have as much knowledge as possible in this field as possible. [[This training will improve PPPs.]] I hope so, because the control bodies must have knowledge, too. Besides, the CBA officers were very well prepared for this training. On the other hand, as far as the provisions on the promotion of public-private partnerships are concerned, we have in Article. 16a. 2 entries indicating that the responsibility of the Minister responsible for PPPs will be inter alia. dissemination and promotion of good practices and analysis of the state and financial perspectives of the PPP sector in Poland. So we also have these records. As regards the actual activities to promote and approximate PPPs, there are provisions on certification, i.e. a public entity that will be interested in carrying out a given project in the PPP formula, it will be able to ask the Ministry of Investment and Development to issue a certificate of the project and experts will examine the validity of the project, the economic and financial aspect. So this will certainly be helpful in preparing PPP projects and will give greater certainty, in particular to local authorities, as to the appropriateness of the chosen way of implementing a given project in the PPP formula. The next element is the formulas of contracts. The Ministry of Investment and Development is currently preparing draft agreements, because one of the reasons for the low effectiveness of concluding negotiations with the agreement is that often the partners, when entering into negotiations, do not know exactly what they are trying to do, and at the moment this effectiveness is only twenty-six percent. For the purposes of government policy on the development of public-private partnerships in Poland, there is an indication that at least 40% the negotiations started were successful. I am convinced that developing model contracts will make the partners know from the very beginning what framework they operate in. Of course, these models of contracts will be voluntary, not mandatory, but I am convinced that at least in terms of estimation and risk sharing, it will be from the very beginning - at least, though - you know how this can be done. The next question concerned the interpretation of VAT and the introduction of regulations in this area into the Public-Private Partnership Act. I wanted to say that in December 2017. The Ministry of Finance has issued interpretations on VAT and at present there is no doubt about VAT settlement. These interpretations from a private investor's point of view are beneficial. There are no longer, as before, different interpretations, and some of them spoke of the necessity of a de facto double payment of VAT: once when the investment was finished within the meaning of the construction process, it was insectified and then the availability fees were charged if it was carried out in the form of accessibility charges. At present, VAT is only paid once and this is clear from this interpretation. The following question concerned the reasons why there were fewer pending proceedings in 2016 and, in fact, in 2017. I would like to point out that the amendment of the Law on concessions was being examined in 2016 and 2017. Due to this process, some entities abstained from the initiation of proceedings, because most public-private partnerships in Poland are concluded on the basis of the law on works concessions or services. There was also a question about data for the first half of 2018. We do not have such data at present - it is 4 July - but we are monitoring the PPP market and in the coming weeks we will have such data. We have set up monitoring on a six-month basis, with two sets of data published on our website as part of market promotion and monitoring. One set is a list of existing public-private partnerships. On this list there are 117 existing contracts worth less than PLN 6 billion. The second list, on the other hand, is potential projects in the form of public-private partnerships. There are currently one hundred and fifty projects on this list, of which at least a few dozen are already in the process of selecting a private partner. This list is treated as a public sector offer to the private sector, which is also a big promotional element when potential private companies, potential partners in one place can find the entire public sector offer, covering both the local government sector and the government sector. At the same time, we wanted to point out that among others. Thanks to our actions, the quality of the proceedings is improved and more procedures end with the conclusion of the agreement. The next question was quite detailed, concerning the proceedings in Żywiec and the reasons why this procedure has not been completed so far. We will try to investigate this case and respond in detail. On the other hand, the revision of the Public-Private Partnership Act and a number of other laws, nineteen, is aimed at removing barriers to the development of public-private partnerships. I hope that such situations and lengthy proceedings will be less and less. Another question was the bad atmosphere around the public-private partnership. It seems to me that here we are partly based on the common understanding of public-private partnership rather than on the statutory understanding. In the common sense, any economic agreement which combines the public and private sectors is understood as a public-private partnership and is not in the statutory sense. Indeed, in this connection, especially in the 1990s, there were a number of agreements that caused these negative stereotypes, but in my opinion they were largely overcome. In the Responsible Development Strategy in the area: Development capital is a special subsection dedicated to the public-private partnership, with a clear indication that public-private partnership is an equivalent source of funding for our country's development, in line with budgetary resources, grant funds or other sources of funding. Thus, already in the most important strategy paper is a clear indication of the development of public-private partnerships. In July Ub. The government adopted a public-private partnership development policy setting out very ambitious objectives for its development. It has been pointed out that by 2020 100 new public-private partnerships are to be concluded, at least 10 government-private partnerships are to be launched and 40% are to be launched. the proceedings initiated are to end with the conclusion of a contract. Fourth objective, most ambitious - 5% Public budget expenditure is to be implemented in 2020 through the implementation of the public-private partnership formula. The government is therefore trying to create the best possible atmosphere for the development of public-private partnerships. It also served to re-activate the Public-Private Partnership Team, which, with broad consensus, with the use of public-private partnership experts active on the market, with the participation of local governments, the government sphere, developed the principles of government policy in the development of public-private partnerships. Thus, this policy was created with a broad social consensus and broad arrangements. ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-04-sejm-66-2.u394	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I am pleased to present the report of the Joint Committees: the Committee on Public Finance, the Committee on Foreign Affairs and the Committee on Justice and Human Rights, on the Government's draft Act on the ratification of the Third Protocol to the General Agreement on the Privileges and Immunities of the Council of Europe, drawn up at Strasbourg on 6 March 1959 and contained in print No 2632. The bill entered the Sejm on 11 May this year. and concerns the determination of the legal situation of the assets, assets and activities of the Council of Europe Development Bank and the legal status of the Bank's bodies and its officers. As Minister Piotr Walczak said at the joint committee meeting, ratification of this protocol is important from the point of view of the Council of Europe Development Bank, which also provides loans to Poland. Since Poland's accession in 1998, it approved loans to Polish entities for a total amount of nearly PLN 5 billion. Poland is one of the main borrowers of the Bank. Among other things, local governments and banks and financial institutions benefit from funding. The Council of Europe Development Bank was involved, among others. in projects for the promotion of rent building, restructuring of coal mining in the Silesian region and currently contributing to the financing of flood protection of the Oder and Vistula basin. As part of its ratification, Poland intends to reserve that it reserves the right not to guarantee exemption from tax on income derived from interest on bonds or loans issued by the Bank. This is due to our desire to reduce tax concessions and to treat entities and institutions operating in the country equally and not to undermine the State Treasury as an issuer of securities. The commitment to the Third Protocol shall not have direct financial consequences. Indirect effects may arise in the event of redemptions and reimbursements of indirect taxes included in the price purchased by the Bank of goods and services. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The Commissions: Public Finance, Foreign Affairs and Justice and Human Rights, following their first reading and examination of the project on 13 June 2018, shall request that the draft Print Act No 2546 annexed to the report be approved without amendment. Thank you very much.
