ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-04-sejm-66-2.u398	Thank you, Marshal. The proposed law aims at agreeing to ratify the Third Protocol to the General Agreement on the privileges and immunities of the Council of Europe under the ratification procedure, in accordance with Article. Article 89 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 points 3 and 5 of the Constitution. The Council of Europe Development Bank provides funding for projects of high social importance in the Member States and related technical knowledge, actively promoting social cohesion and strengthening social inclusion in Europe. It is an important instrument of solidarity policy in Europe, participates in the financing of social projects, responds to crisis situations and contributes to improving living conditions in the Member States. The Bank is implementing investment projects through three sectoral orientations: sustainable and inclusive growth, the integration of refugees, displaced persons and migrants and climate action. It was originally created as a humanitarian instrument for helping refugees, but the growing diversity of Member States' needs in the social field has led to an extension of the scope of action both geographically and sectorally. Thus, while assistance for refugees, migrants and victims of natural or environmental disasters remains a statutory priority of the Council of Europe Development Bank, it is now increasingly contributing to strengthening social cohesion in Europe by supporting investment projects targeted, among others. supporting housing, creating, maintaining sustainable jobs, improving living conditions in rural and urban areas. The rapporteur mentioned that a very large part of the Bank for the Development of the Council of Europe focuses on Poland. I will mention here examples of the river basin or coal mining. Ratification by Poland of the protocol already in force means that the Republic of Poland will become its party, and all the more so should we do so. As parliamentarians, we have an obligation to play an active role in promoting the initiatives of the Council of Europe Development Bank at national level, and joining the signatories of the third protocol will provide a certificate of Poland's integration and cooperation with the Member States within the Council of Europe. In view of the above, joining the important mission carried out by the Council of Europe Development Bank should be a pure formality for Poland. Therefore, on behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Platform states that we wish to support this project. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-04-sejm-66-2.u405	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The draft amendment of the Water Law Act results from several months of experience in implementing Water Law and Labour of Polish Waters since 1 January this year. The project takes into account the voices of entities cooperating with Polish Waters, mainly entrepreneurs. The amendment is a strictly sequencing and reducing burden, mainly administrative, on the part of economic operators. First of all, we are converting the form of water management control from the charging system to the quarterly declaration of entrepreneurs, which will significantly facilitate the collection of fees, but also reduce the control burden on these operators. In addition, we depart from charging up to 20 PLN so that we do not initiate proceedings and generate additional costs. One of the most important changes is the possibility of continuing existing contracts with entities without the need for a tender. This makes it particularly efficient for tourism services during the tourist season, when cooperation can continue. In addition, it is important to remove the obligation to have a water permit in the case of temporary locating, up to 180 days, construction facilities in areas of special risk of flooding, e.g. a box of fries or other small objects that are found in a flooded belt. From other major changes we add a provision that will result in the obligation to submit a single dossier. In the event that water permit activities cover the scope of several regions, one single dossier can be submitted in one place and several applications cannot be dealt with in parallel. And a slight change: removing the obligation related to the State Water Management of Polish Waters to publish in the local press information about the initiation of proceedings, which will reduce costs, but also speed up proceedings. There is also a provision ordering the prohibition of rainwater entering groundwater and the prohibition of rainwater containing substances particularly harmful to water equipment. These are the fundamental changes. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-04-sejm-66-2.u430	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! I present the position of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle towards the government's draft law amending the Water Law Act and some other laws. This draft law highlights important legal solutions that favour the reduction of administrative formalities in the scope of promises or legal consents. In addition, the draft law limits certain requirements, such as the submission of documents or data that the body already has in its databases. This will significantly reduce red tape and reduce some long-term administrative procedures. There are many changes to the articles in this Act. These changes affect the essence of the Water Law, the improvement of which is required. Many of these changes will lead to very important effects. The proposed law is compatible with European Union law and will have an important impact on the activities of micro-entrepreneurs and medium-sized or small enterprises. The effects that will be achieved after the introduction of this Act will significantly improve many important activities carried out by companies. It will be worth mentioning the effects of the amendments to the Articles of this Act: to eliminate interpretation doubts related to the operation of linear infrastructure in the case of the designation of a shoreline, to prohibit the introduction of rainwater into groundwater and to prohibit the introduction of rainwater containing substances which are particularly harmful to water equipment, to clarify the interpretation doubts regarding the application of rules on the management of buildings and land-use in areas at risk of flooding. Standards will be introduced according to which water facilities which have either been made by the Polish Waters on land covered by inland waters flowing as well as on the property of the State Treasury will be the property of the State Treasury. Contracts will continue without a call for tender, which is another element in reducing bureaucracy. Complaint procedures will be improved in many different cases. Another effect will be to set the minimum threshold charged for water services and to optimise the charging system for water services for power plants and power stations with open cooling circuits. Changes to the Act will result in voivodeships being the data sources for the water management information system. Another effect will be to establish a number of regulations related to waste management. The transfer of competence in water law assessments to directors of regional water management boards will also have a significant impact on improving water law activities. Another important effect will be the standardization of the system of measurement of tariff meters. This is to be based on credibility, which will serve legal and modern measuring instruments. The changes I have mentioned will make a very important contribution to the efficiency and reduction of bureaucracy in relation to the Water Law in our country. Therefore, the Free and Solidarnia Circle supports these amendments. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-04-sejm-66-2.u436	Thank you. On behalf of the Civic Platform Club, the position will be presented by Mrs Zofia Czernow. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-04-sejm-66-2.u437	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! Minister! I have the honour to present the position of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club on the government draft law on the National Debt Register, print No. 2637. The draft law shall constitute the implementation referred to in Article. 5 of the Act of 15 May 2015. Restructuring Law to create on 1 February 2018. Central Register of Restructuring and Impairment. The purpose of this register was to streamline restructuring and bankruptcy proceedings, to facilitate access to information on these proceedings, to improve communication between the authorities and their participants and to reduce the costs of proceedings. This register was not created, while work was being carried out on the extension of its scope. The result of these works is this draft law. The scope of the National Debt Register significantly goes beyond the regulations of the Restructuring Law and insolvency law and will be broader than originally assumed to be the source of information about insolvent entities at risk of insolvency or those subject to unsuccessful execution. This required the repeal of the said Article. 5 The Law on Restructuring and the adoption of a separate law, which at the same time changes to a broad extent the Act on Bankruptcy and the Law on Restructuring. The aim is to build a single IT system that will handle insolvency and restructuring proceedings and the National Debt Register. The register shall disclose information about natural persons, legal persons and organisational units which are not legal persons in respect of which restructuring, insolvency proceedings are or have been conducted, the decision to prohibit, the recognition of the decision to open foreign insolvency proceedings, the shareholders of commercial companies who are liable for the obligations of the company without limitation of all their assets if the bankruptcy of the company, the natural persons, legal persons and organisational units not legal persons to whom enforcement proceedings conducted by the bailiff have been terminated, and the natural persons to whom the enforcement of maintenance benefits is pending for a period of more than 3 months are committed. The register is open and everyone has the right to read the data contained in it. The general principle is the non-removalability of data from the register. The disclosure of the data in the register shall be automatically discontinued 10 years after the date of the final termination or termination of the proceedings. The three-year period concerns a legally approved agreement. When analysing the bill, it should be stressed that such a document, which would contain all the necessary information on restructuring or bankruptcy processes in the ICT system, is very much needed, among others. to increase the safety of trade. However, there are many doubts about the detailed solutions contained in the bill. One of them is the protection of personal data, especially GDPR. The question is whether the publication of the PESEL number constitutes an excessive and disproportionate interference in the rights and freedoms of persons who are to be found in the National Debt Register. These and other reservations and doubts should be clarified in the course of further legislative work, of which I am continuing to request on behalf of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

But it's not about stigmatization, but it's about the safety of turnover, it's about the fact that contracting with people who are de facto incapable of paying off liabilities is insanely risky. This applies, of course, to professional turnover, to entrepreneurs, but it also applies to normal people who make commitments, they already have debts and these debts increase. Therefore, this is necessary - and not only do we see it as the creator of the project, but we are talking about the implementation of European legislation - so that knowledge about debt holders is widely available, as much as possible, so that it is not that we contract in good faith in professional or consumer trade with someone who is not about to comply or will not be able to meet their obligations because it already has this excess of debt over assets. So it's a question of marketing security, because it's just as important or more important than some people get into financial trouble without their own fault. It's about marketing safety. It is not as if some of your fellow Members have said, that this is a complete novelty, that this has so far been classified, and now we reveal that this has no basis. We have a record of insolvent debtors. Not many people know about this because it's not very popular. Maybe the tool is wrong, maybe the normative basis is archaic, but there is a register of insolvent debtors in the National Court Register, where failing entrepreneurs are registered, are entered debtors who have been found to be ineffective in the execution of a private or public creditor's application. Why this seven-year period? This thread actually appears in every expression. It is because in the case of a register of insolvent debtors, these 7 years are already accepted today and so far no one has specifically questioned it. We're making a new register, modern. We hope that much more friendly and accessible from the point of view of seeking information about insolvent persons, but we are not making some revolutionary change here. There was this period of 7 years, there was the principle of public access to these data, including sensitive data. There's nothing new here. What's new besides some technical changes? There is a Europe-wide requirement that we need to adapt to so that there is a system across Europe that makes it possible to disclose the data of insolvent people. Of course, in this system we do not need to disclose the so-called. Alimentaries, but it's our choice that we want to expose them because we think it's highly reprehensible that so many people don't pay to support their children first of all. So if the register is easily accessible, the public will learn to use it. There will also be data on so-called. All right, all right, all right, all right, all right, all right, all right, all right, all right, all right, all right, And that's very good, maybe it'll be another measure that will force more timely regulation of maintenance obligations. Ms Czernow's question about GDPR. There can be no conflict with the GDPR, because the GDPR already takes into account at EU level as an act that we are trying to implement here with the canoe - it works better or worse, but we are trying, we are not the only ones in Europe who have a problem with it, but this is an EU act which forces various types of restrictions - EU law on the obligation to create a register of restructuring and bankruptcy, which we have expanded and called the National Debt Register. Thus, there can be no non-compliance at national level, since Union legislation takes into account the existence of an exemption from the general principle of the protection of personal data of those situations which concern insolvent debtors. At Union level there is no right to be non-compliance and there is no such non-compliance at national level. And there's no such discrepancy, or at least there's no indication that such non-compliance would occur. Of course, PESEL is an identification number that clearly identifies a person, whether an entrepreneur or any natural person. It is not that we now introduce an obligation to disclose PESEL, and that obligation was not. Look who's interested in this article. 51 Bankruptcy rights, ours, in force. As regards the declaration of bankruptcy of natural persons, entrepreneurs shall be reported PESEL. Art. 491 concerns natural persons, but consumers - PESEL is given, address, place of residence is given. All this data is published in the Court and Economic Monitor. The fact that few people use it or even know that there is a Judicial and Economic Monitor is a matter of reality, but we turn the archaic advertising system into an MSiG. This is a modern system, but these announcements that are already publicly available today, only very few who are interested in it, will now be publicly available, only in a much more modern register. We do not change philosophy, it is already, but it was probably noticed on the occasion of work on this law by parliamentarians, by those who submitted various kinds of comments in the course of the public consultation. In Mrs Fabisiak's speech there is a GDPR, trendy, now everyone is talking about it, but there is no way there is a contradiction here. If we see in the course of the legislative process that such a contradiction exists somewhere, we are obviously ready to discuss it, but as of today outside the GDPR's motto, we have not heard any concrete complaint that could be made to our project. What took you so long, seven years? That's all there is. Unfortunately, even if someone paid off the debt, for some time this information about the repayment of the debt should be available, which means that it was the person to whom the contractual risk existed. These seven years have been in the RDN, and that's how we leave it. We are very concerned, especially in the context of the discussion on GDPR, about these issues of transparency. In Scandinavia, all tax returns are public, not only property statements made by parliamentarians, judges and other selected groups. Nobody gets excited about it. There's identification numbers, there's addresses, and there's nothing going on. No great crime was found using this data, much more accurate, much more detailed. There society is used to being more public, not with us, but what we are proposing is really nothing in terms of European law or certain common sense rules. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-04-sejm-66-2.u455	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present on behalf of the club of the Polish People's Party - Union of European Democrats our position on the government draft law on the National Debt Register, print No. 2637. This project assumes the creation of the National Debt Register. It will be public, which means that everyone will be able to read the data revealed in it via the Internet. The government proposal also envisages facilitating the restructuring and bankruptcy of entrepreneurs. This is a response to the important problems of the restructuring and bankruptcy courts resulting from a significant increase in the number of cases relating in particular to consumer bankruptcy. Projectors propose to introduce an obligation to submit applications and documents by electronic means in the restructuring and bankruptcy proceedings of entrepreneurs - this will enable the automatic transfer of data from the application submitted by electronic means to repertoires and the court's and the judge's rules on the matter. An electronic service is introduced as a principle in the project, which is intended to significantly speed up the communication of the participants in the proceedings with the court. From this principle, an exception has been proposed to allow applications to be submitted outside the electronic system - workers, maintenance creditors and consumers' debtors will be able to submit paper applications - and orally - in the application office of each court. The files of restructuring and bankruptcy proceedings are to be kept in electronic form from now on. Access to them will be possible both in the file reader's room in court and through the created public internet portal. The draft also introduces an obligation to submit letters on forms set out in the electronic system, which will be mandatory for participants and bodies of the proceedings. An important change is also the creation of a single account for the entity, which will allow for communication with the court in restructuring and bankruptcy cases and registered cases. With the entry into force of the proposed law, the obligation to publish notices in the Court and Economic Monitor will disappear. This is expected to bring savings of PLN 3.6 million per year to the interested. The newly created National Debt Register will disclose the data of persons subject to restructuring and insolvency proceedings and those subject to ineffective execution. The project assumes that the National Debt Register will also be visited by people who are behind with alimony payments. The aim here is to increase the collection of alimony, since the register will contain information on the persons subject to the execution of maintenance benefits and whose arrears exceed 3 months. High Izbo! The proposed changes should be assessed positively. They will help to save businesses and reduce the costs of proceedings. Less paper correspondence and faster circulation of documents will allow for better functioning of courts. An important aspect of the project is also to facilitate access to court files and significantly reduce the time needed for access to the data contained in the documents on restructuring and bankruptcy proceedings. It is also important to emphasise the European dimension of the changes being made. The new system will combine bankruptcy registers and the European e-Justice portal, which will act as a central electronic public access point for information in the system. The system will offer a search function in all official languages of the Union institutions to allow access to mandatory information and any other documents and information contained in insolvency registers maintained by Member States, which will also decide to make their resources available via the e-Justice portal.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-04-sejm-66-2.u457	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! My statement concerns the commemoration of the life and work of the great painter and poet Wlastimil Hofman. The year of Wlastimil Hofman in Szklarska Poręba ended very solemnly. The city, inhabitants of the entire region and numerous tourists celebrated artistic events specially prepared for this extraordinary anniversary. Wlastimil Hofman, born in Prague in a Polish-Czech family, spent his entire life in Poland, including many years until his death, lived in Szklarska Poręba. During his studies of painting at the School of Fine Arts and at the beginning of his creative work he lived in Krakow, the most important place for Polish culture at that time, during the most colourful period of Young Poland. The fame of the young Hofman brought the painting of a confession made in 1906 in Zachęta. Shortly after that, Wlastimil Hofman was the first Pole to be nominated as a member of the Vienna Secession. After the war wandering in 1947 he chose Szklarska Poręba as his place for life and creativity. In painting he continued the symbolic and allegorical line initiated by Jack Malczewski. He was also a follower of the rich tradition of the artistic colony in Szklarska Poręba. He had a warm relationship with residents and tourists who often posed him for the paintings for which he had been close and friendly. He mainly painted portraits, but also paintings about ancient, fairytale and religious themes. From under his brush came such works as a series of 30 paintings, our Father, the Four Evangelists, the cycle of the Rosary, the Triptych, the Adoration of the Child, the Eucharistic Christ and others. His paintings adorn the altars of many churches. Permanently lived and created in a small wooden house called Vlastimilówka today. Hofman was also a poet. Beautiful and touching are his poems about religious themes, but also about the beauty of the Krkonoše and surrounding nature, which he constantly admired. Hofman's paintings are found in all the most important collections of Polish paintings. Especially numerous are the collections of paintings in the National Museums in Cracow, Poznań and Warsaw, but also in the houses of the Franciscan Fathers of the Cracow Province. A large collection of paintings, sketches, drawings, photographs and letters of the painter is located in the Krkonoše Museum in Jelenia Góra. The selection of images from the collection can be seen in the museum of Carl and Gerhart Hauptmann in Szklarska Poręba. Hofman's canvases have a large number of private collectors throughout Poland and abroad. Hofman considered himself a Polish painter and was perceived as such. Today Wlastimilówka is a private property in which there is a museum filled with the artist's spirit, reflecting the climate of space and time. The city authorities have decided to honor the life and work of an outstanding artist by establishing the Year of Wlastimil Hofman. A series of exhibition events, artistic workshops, tours in the footsteps of the artist, discussions, poetic evenings and painting outdoors inspired by the artist's work were organized. The inhabitants of Szklarska Poręba are proud that an outstanding painter chose their town for his small homeland, loved this place and lived among them as a friend. Wlastimil Hofman rests on the municipal cemetery in Szklarska Poręba. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-04-sejm-66-2.u461	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On 11 April this year, the Civic Committee of the legislative initiative was registered by the Marshal of the Sejm, whose purpose is to collect signatures under the law amending the Law on the Prevention and Control of Immortal Diseases. The fundamental objective of this law and its amendments is to abolish the compulsory preventive vaccination, i.e. those that concern children, as is the case in 20 European countries, of course civilized countries, countries such as Germany and many, many others. At the same time, this initiative has led to very unpleasant attacks on those collecting signatures under this law, e.g. They are called so-called. Anti-vaccines. I would like to state at this point very strongly that they are not anti-vaccines, at least I do not know anything about such and I personally have not met any such person in my life. The change is intended, as I said, to introduce voluntary vaccination in the case of children. This concerns an amendment to the provision of the Article. 17 Act on the prevention and control of infectious diseases, while the project does not change Article. 33 of the Act, which introduces the so-called. vaccination, necessary in the event of an outbreak or emergency. Ladies and gentlemen! In addition to this basic objective, i.e. voluntary vaccination in children, the aim of this bill is the following: firstly, the main aim is the voluntary vaccination which I said 20 European countries, the citizens of these countries, secondly, the thorough medical history and written information about the risk before vaccination, thirdly, the thorough medical history after vaccination, then the possibility for parents to report adverse vaccination reactions and judicial supervision of the register of these reactions, and finally the possibility to comment on changes to the vaccination calendar. Ladies and gentlemen! For unknown reasons, Poland is a country in which an enormous number of forced vaccines are still maintained, to the extent that the child receives three compulsory vaccines in the first day of his life, in the first day of his birth. I know from court practice, because I go to cases related to this problem, that very often there is not even a proper medical interview that could rule out possible contraindications. This results in undesirable vaccine reactions and various health complications and even permanent health damage. This cannot be the case, and the right to whether or not to vaccinate children should, of course, belong to parents. As I said, this is happening in 20 European countries. Then, too, the problem is that parents do not have the opportunity to report possible adverse vaccine reactions on their own, as the doctor does. However, the problem is that the doctor has some sort of conflict of interest, because it is he who qualifies for vaccination. If it happens that his diagnosis before vaccination is not very accurate, he will not really have any interest in reporting this reaction. Then the parents are not able to, in fact, have a judicial remedy in relation to the reporting of this type of adverse vaccine reaction. It's all about eliminating this bill. So once again I stress: It is not intended to combat vaccines or vaccinations as such, but to allow parents to give them the right to choose whether they want their children, especially the smallest ones, to vaccinate or not. And it's not a strange solution, it's not some sort of departure from the prey of civilization, but, on the contrary, it's giving parents the right to choose. Especially since there is no compensation system in Poland related to the adverse effects of vaccination. If there's a forced vaccination, there should be a compensation system. There's obviously no such thing. Ladies and gentlemen! If we want to be a civilized country, we cannot treat people as breeding animals, and this is how we treat Polish children at the moment - you apply vaccines to them whether parents want it or not, and whether there are real indications or not. If we want to treat people as free and responsible, this need for preventive vaccination must, of course, be abolished, which in no way reduces the safety of society or the population in its entirety. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
